## Sheet: `Sick Leave Paid By Employer`

| Guaranteed Salary During Incapacity for Work - Paid by the Employer (Belgium, 2026) |  |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Employment Contracts Act of 3 July 1978, arts. 52, 54, 70 and following. Belgium replaces the employer's obligation with a public benefit after a short employer-funded period: once the guaranteed salary ends, the health insurance fund (under the National Institute for Health and Disability Insurance) pays 60% of the CAPPED gross during the first year of incapacity. |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |
| Population | Days 1 to 7 | Days 8 to 14 | Days 15 to 30 | From day 31 |
| White-collar (bediende) on an indefinite contract or a contract of at least 3 months | 100% of normal pay | 100% of normal pay | 100% of normal pay | Health insurance fund: 60% of the capped gross during the first year of incapacity |
| Blue-collar (arbeider), after 1 month of uninterrupted service | 100% of normal pay | 85.88% of the gross | 25.88% of the gross up to the ceiling PLUS 85.88% of the part above the ceiling; the health insurance fund pays the balance | Health insurance fund: 60% of the capped gross during the first year of incapacity |
|  |  |  |  |  |
| Guaranteed salary period: 30 CALENDAR days at the employer's expense. |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |
| TWO CHANGES TOOK EFFECT ON 1 JANUARY 2026 AND MUST BE CONFIGURED |  |  |  |  |
| 1. RELAPSE PERIOD EXTENDED FROM 14 DAYS TO 8 WEEKS. Where an employee falls sick again within 8 weeks of a previous incapacity, NO new guaranteed salary period starts, unless the balance of the previous period is unused or the employee proves a different illness. (Law of 19 December 2025 implementing a reinforced return-to-work policy for incapacity for work.) |  |  |  |  |
| 2. NO GUARANTEED SALARY IS DUE for an incapacity arising during a period of PROGRESSIVE WORK RESUMPTION. The former 20-week limit on neutralising guaranteed salary during progressive work resumption was removed by the same law. |  |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |  |
| RELATED RULES |  |  |  |  |
| Topic | Rule |  |  |  |
| Sickness during annual leave | Since 1 January 2024 an employee who falls sick during a period of annual leave KEEPS the leave days: the days convert into sickness days, the guaranteed salary applies, and the leave days are recovered and may be taken later, including after 31 December where they could not be taken (Law of 17 July 2023). |  |  |  |
| Social security and tax treatment | Guaranteed salary is continued pay by the employer: it is explicitly WITHIN the NSSO wage concept (13.07% employee and ordinary employer contributions) and is taxed at the ordinary Annex III scale. It is not an exceptional payment. |  |  |  |
| Holiday accrual | Sickness within the guaranteed salary period is an EQUIVALENT DAY under the Royal Decree of 30 March 1967 and therefore PRESERVES holiday accrual and the double holiday pay base. Ordinary unpaid leave is not equivalent and reduces both. |  |  |  |
| Employer top-up | Any employer or sectoral supplement paid on top of the health insurance fund benefit after the guaranteed salary period is ORDINARY taxable and contributory wage (wage type 62190). The exemption attaching to the statutory benefit does not extend to it. |  |  |  |
| DmfA and ASR | Payroll must stop the salary, produce the correct DmfA absence code and, where applicable, the electronic scenario declaration (ASR - DRS scenario for incapacity) that triggers the benefit. The DmfA absence code drives both the employee's benefit entitlement and the holiday accrual. |  |  |  |
| Wage types in this catalogue | 62960 white-collar days 1-30 (100%); 62961 blue-collar days 1-7 (100%); 62962 blue-collar days 8-14 (85.88%); 62963 blue-collar days 15-30 (25.88% / 85.88% split); 21108 salary reduction once the guaranteed salary period ends; 21190 salary reduction for long-term incapacity; 62190 employer supplement; 62968 guaranteed salary following a work accident. |  |  |  |

## Sheet: `Tax Credit`

| Tax Credits and Withholding Reductions (Belgium, income year 2026) |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Belgium delivers relief almost entirely as a REDUCTION OF THE WITHHOLDING, not as a reduction of the taxable base. The only genuine pre-tax deduction is the compulsory 13.07% employee social security contribution (ITC 92 art. 52, 3 degrees). The withholding is a payment on account: no year-end true-up is performed in payroll and the definitive position is settled in the personal income tax assessment. |  |  |  |
|  |  |  |  |
| Relief | Amount / rate (income year 2026) | Eligibility | Application point |
| Fiscal work bonus (fiscale werkbonus) | Maximum EUR 765 per calendar year, NOT indexed, applied from income year 2025. Computed as a percentage of the social work bonus granted in the period, capped on an ANNUAL basis. Reform phase-in (NOT CONFIRMED AS ENACTED - do not treat as settled): a reinforcement is planned, but a March-2026 source states it had not yet been definitively adopted by the Chamber at that date, and government messaging describes the goal more loosely (reinforcement from 2028, gross=net target for the lowest earners by 2029) than a fixed 2027 schedule. Do not configure the specific 2,000/63%/72% figures without a fresh primary-source check. | Employees ACTUALLY RECEIVING the social work bonus. No employee declaration - it is derived entirely from the social work bonus. | Direct reduction of the computed withholding. Reported on 281.10 in box 284 (zone 2.115) for the part qualifying for the 33.14% tax credit and box 360 (zone 2.210) for the 52.54% part. |
| Social work bonus (sociale werkbonus) - the trigger | TWO components since 1 April 2024, computed separately and added (P = R_B + R_A); each pays its maximum only at or below its own full-amount (lower) limit and is degressive between that limit and its own zero point. Component B (bediende/white-collar): full amount up to EUR 2,218.73 (1 Jan and 1 Mar) -> EUR 2,255.50 (1 Apr) -> EUR 2,300.62 (1 Jul and 1 Sep); maximum EUR 165.87 -> EUR 168.62 -> EUR 171.99; coefficient 0.2699 throughout; reaches zero at EUR 2,833.36 -> EUR 2,880.32 -> EUR 2,937.93. Component A (bediende/white-collar): full amount up to EUR 2,833.36 (1 Jan and 1 Mar) -> EUR 2,880.32 (1 Apr) -> EUR 2,937.93 (1 Jul and 1 Sep); maximum EUR 123.00 -> EUR 125.04 -> EUR 127.54; coefficient 0.2807 (1 Jan) -> 0.2442 (1 Mar) -> 0.2738 (1 Apr) -> 0.3196 (1 Jul) -> 0.2739 (1 Sep); reaches zero at EUR 3,271.48 (1 Jan) -> EUR 3,336.98 (1 Mar, unchanged 1 Apr and 1 Jul) -> EUR 3,403.62 (1 Sep). Component B's zero point is always component A's full-amount limit, so the two dovetail. The blue-collar (arbeider) maxima AND coefficients are the white-collar figures x 1.08 (e.g. from 1 Jul 2026 EUR 185.75 / 0.2915 and EUR 137.74 / 0.3452) because the contribution runs on the 108% base; the wage limits are identical for both categories, S being the wage at 100%. The parameter set steps five times in 2026 - select the row by the pay month. From 1 July 2026 the combined maximum is EUR 299.53 white-collar (EUR 171.99 + EUR 127.54) and EUR 323.49 blue-collar (EUR 185.75 + EUR 137.74). Annual cap EUR 3,466.44 from 1 January 2026, EUR 3,523.92 from 1 April 2026 and EUR 3,594.36 from 1 July 2026, the last closed on 16 September 2026. NSSO administrative instructions edition 2026/3, the werkbonus annex: 'het totaal van de vermindering per werknemer niet meer bedragen dan 3.594,36 EUR per kalenderjaar'. Cross-checked against the component maxima, which reconcile exactly: EUR 171.99 (component B) + EUR 127.54 (component A) = EUR 299.53 per month white-collar, x 12 = EUR 3,594.36. This matters for the fiscal work bonus because that credit is a percentage of the social work bonus ACTUALLY GRANTED, at different rates per component (33.14% on component A and 52.54% on component B), so an understated social work bonus understates the fiscal one too. | Low-wage employees. PRORATED by the fraction of full-time performance, so a half-time employee receives half the maximum. Applied by EACH employer on its own reference wage, which can produce an over-granted bonus that the NSSO recovers. | Reduces the employee 13.07% contribution, never below zero. It cannot create a negative deduction. It is the gateway to the fiscal work bonus above. |
| Overtime relief - employee reduction | 66.81% of the BASE on which a 50% or 100% overtime supplement is calculated; 57.75% where the supplement is 20%. The relief is computed on the BASE, not on the supplement itself. | Employees who worked overtime carrying a statutory supplement, up to 180 HOURS per employee per calendar year - MADE PERMANENT FROM 2026. Higher ceilings in construction with electronic attendance registration and in hotels and catering with a registered cash register. Managerial staff outside the working time rules are excluded. | Reduction of the withholding on the payslip. Base reported on 281.10 in box 233 (zone 2.067, hours in 2.095) for 66.81% and box 234 (zone 2.068, hours in 2.097) for 57.75%. Hour counter in box 305 (zone 2.142). Hours above the ceiling are taxed normally. |
| Overtime relief - employer exemption | 41.25% exemption from REMITTING the withholding computed on the base of a 50% or 100% supplement; 32.19% where the supplement is 20% (ITC 92 art. 275/1). | Same 180-hour ceiling and the same populations as the employee reduction. The employer must keep the overtime records. | Reduction of the amount REMITTED in the monthly withholding tax return through Finprof. An employer cash advantage: it does NOT change the employee's net or the amount shown as withheld on the 281.10. |
| Personal supplementary pension contribution | Withholding reduction of 30% of the contribution withheld. No separate payroll cap; the plan itself is constrained by the 80% rule. | Employees affiliated to a second-pillar plan providing for a personal contribution. Requires affiliation and consent to the deduction. | The contribution is withheld from NET pay; the relief is a reduction of the COMPUTED WITHHOLDING under Annex III - it is NOT a reduction of the taxable base. Reported on 281.10 in box 285 (zone 2.081); box 283 for individual continuation and box 387 for the VAPW. |
| Basic tax-free allowance and dependent-child relief | Basic allowance EUR 11,170 (Annex III nr. 32), delivered as a reduction of EUR 2,987.98. Dependent-child relief is a SEPARATE flat euro reduction of the basisbelasting (Annex III nr. 38), NOT a supplement to the allowance: EUR 624 for one dependent child, EUR 1,656 for two, EUR 4,404 for three, EUR 7,620 for four, EUR 11,100 for five, EUR 14,592 for six, EUR 18,120 for seven, EUR 21,996 for eight, plus EUR 3,864 per child above the eighth; a disabled dependent child counts as two. Separate reductions apply for a disabled employee, a disabled dependant, a dependent ascendant aged 65 or over, and a single parent. | Residents, and non-residents meeting the 75% condition. NOT granted under scale III. Where the employee does not declare the family situation, apply scale I with no dependants - the least favourable lawful position. | Two distinct steps: the basic allowance is delivered as a reduction of the annual tax equal to 11,170 x 26.75% = EUR 2,987.98 (step 7), and the flat child amount is then subtracted from the resulting basisbelasting (step 8). The employer must not apply an unsubstantiated claim: the risk falls on the employer as withholding agent. |
| Lump-sum professional expenses | 30% of the annual gross taxable income, CAPPED AT EUR 6,070. Formula: min(30% x annual taxable income; 6,070). | All employees. An employee may instead prove actual expenses in the annual return, which payroll never applies. | Deducted from the annualised taxable income at step 4 of the withholding calculation, before the scale is applied. |
| Special social security contribution - NO relief | Maximum EUR 182.82 per quarter (individual assessment) or EUR 154.92 (joint assessment with a spouse having professional income). | All employees within the bands. | RECORDED HERE AS A NEGATIVE: the special social security contribution is NOT deductible from the taxable base and gives NO withholding reduction. Configuring it as deductible is a known Belgian defect. |
|  |  |  |  |
| NO YEAR-END TAX ADJUSTMENT IS PERFORMED IN PAYROLL |  |  |  |
| The Belgian withholding is NON-CUMULATIVE: each payment is taxed as though it were the employee's only income, the employer carries no year-to-date tax position, and there is no December true-up and no refund of over-withholding by the employer. The employee recovers any excess through the personal income tax assessment, normally issued in the second half of the year following the income year. The year-end obligations are REPORTING obligations only: issue form 281.10 to each employee and file it with the summary 325.10 through Belcotax-on-web by 28 FEBRUARY following the income year, and reconcile the twelve monthly withholding tax returns to the 281.10 totals before filing. |  |  |  |

They are not repeated here. | Belcotax-on-web fiche 281.10: carried per wage type in Wage Type Catalogue column G (Tax Code), 23 box codes + NA, across 359 rows. The eight other Belgian statutory reports - Dimona, DmfA, DmfA modification, DRS, Finprof 274, work accident declaration, Limosa and the SEPA credit transfer - are specified in BE-compliance/04-reports-spec/ (nine specifications: three at v1.2 - BE-FIN-001, BE-FIN-002 and BE-FEBELFIN-001, effective 16 August 2026; five at v1.3 - BE-NSSO-001, BE-NSSO-002, BE-NSSO-003, BE-NSSO-004 and BE-FEDRIS-001, reissued 24 August 2026 by the ISSUE-B content re-alignment; and BE-NSSO-005 at v1.3 (DESCOPED)). | Maintained in column L (System Mapping) of sheet 4 of each of the nine report specifications in BE-compliance/04-reports-spec/, row by row, and deliberately NOT duplicated on this sheet so that exactly one copy is authoritative. RESOLVED 21 August 2026, ISSUE-2 of BE-QA-REPORT-2026-08-21.md. The 226 rows that named Data Dictionary provenance as a prose category ("Employee master data - street.") now carry a resolvable citation in the form BE-DD-001-v1.4 - 'Field Name' (Sheet Name), the form already used correctly on 318 rows. Column L now resolves to a named Data Dictionary field on 521 rows across the nine specifications, and every one of the 203 citations written - drawn from 75 distinct field names - was asserted to exist on the named sheet of the live BE-DD-001-v1.4 before it was written. 23 of the 226 were RECLASSIFIED rather than cited, because no DD field exists to cite: 19 Limosa rows - 18 whose assignment-specific DD fields were REMOVED from BE-DD-001-v1.4 when that declaration was descoped, plus the Limosa-1 receipt number, which is returned by the channel - the two Dimona C3.2A control-card rows, and two Belcotax rows, the starter-job compensatory supplement and the recognised social secretariat. SEPARATELY, finding BE-X10-011 - BE-WTC-001 references resolving to a Mercans Code in column A - was closed on 17 August 2026: 79 cells across 4 of the 9 specifications carry the Mercans Code alongside the wage-type name, and the rest name the catalogue as a whole or a payroll concept rather than an individual wage type. The figure of 195 unresolved references this cell carried until 21 August 2026 was stale on both counts and is superseded. | Maintained in BE-compliance/05-data-dictionary/BE-DD-001-v1.4.xlsx, sheets Country Fields and Country Option Mappers. BE-DD-001-v1.4's own changelog records an alignment patch taken against BE-WTC-001 from the Data Dictionary side on 14 August 2026. No cross-check has been run from this catalogue's side - see Version Control remark 'BUILD ORDER - OUT OF SEQUENCE'. STAGE 9.5, 16 September 2026 - DmfA CODE TABLES NOW RESOLVABLE FROM HERE. Every wage type on this catalogue is declared on the DmfA under a remuneration code (zone 00067) and its working time under a service code (zone 00062). Both code tables were previously documented nowhere in this dossier. They now live in BE-DD-001-v1.4, sheet Country Option Mappers, as the mapper blocks 'DmfA Remuneration Code' (44 codes, RSZ Bijlage 7) and 'DmfA Service Code' (52 codes, RSZ Bijlage 8), both taken from the NSSO DmfA glossary edition 2026/2. They are held there rather than on this workbook because the WTC contract is six sheets and twenty columns A-T and has no place for a code table, and because this sheet's standing rule is that exactly one copy of a crosswalk is authoritative. The same rule applies here: this cell points, it does not duplicate. | CROSSWALK MAINTAINED ELSEWHERE - not outstanding. Both artefacts exist: the nine Report Specifications (Stage 3) were delivered on 14 August 2026 and re-issued on 16 August 2026, three of them remaining at that v1.2 level (BE-FIN-001, BE-FIN-002, BE-FEBELFIN-001); five were re-issued again at v1.3 on 24 August 2026 (BE-NSSO-001, BE-NSSO-002, BE-NSSO-003, BE-NSSO-004, BE-FEDRIS-001) and BE-NSSO-005-Limosa stands at v1.3 (DESCOPED); the Data Dictionary (Stage 4) was delivered on 14 August 2026 and re-issued as BE-DD-001-v1.3.xlsx on 16 August 2026. The previous contents of this row asserted that neither had been built, which was false from 14 August onwards. Corrected 17 August 2026, Stage 10 cross-artifact fix, finding BE-X10-003. |

## Appendix: build notes of the pipeline workbook (16 September 2026)

*The Drive workbook carries a short Version Control (1.0 Initial Version, 1.1 Global WTC 62/65/79 completion, 1.2 employer rate in row 23671, 1.3 All Codes 62/65/79 completion at rows 829-1183). The pipeline build workbook of 16 September 2026, whose Wage Type Catalogue rows 2-360 are identical to the Drive file, carried the full build history and remarks below. They are kept here verbatim as reference; row numbers they cite are unchanged in v1.3.*

|  | 1 | 2026-08-13 | Initial Belgium Wage Type Catalogue. Built from BE-CCG-001-v1.1 (13 modules, primary-source-verified content review, 8 defects found and fixed) and the Global WTC V1.0 baseline. All 300 global Pay Element Codes configured for Belgium in baseline order, plus 55 Belgium-specific wage types. Income year 2026 / assessment year 2027 parameters throughout. | Draft | Wallisson Gomes |
|  | 1.1 | 2026-09-16 | STAGE 9.5 GAP CLOSURE. (1) SOCIAL WORK BONUS ANNUAL CAP CLOSED. Four cells - Version Control C25, Wage Type Catalogue F303, Tax Credit B6 and this line's predecessors - carried the cap after the 1 July 2026 indexation as an open gap, with an explicit instruction not to extrapolate it from the 2% step applied to the other parameters. That instruction was right and it was obeyed: the figure is now taken from the published NSSO administrative instructions edition 2026/3, which went live after this catalogue was built. The cap is EUR 3,466.44 from 1 January 2026, EUR 3,523.92 from 1 April 2026 and EUR 3,594.36 from 1 July 2026. It reconciles exactly against the component maxima in force from 1 July - EUR 171.99 plus EUR 127.54 is EUR 299.53 a month white-collar, and twelve times that is EUR 3,594.36 - so the two independent statements of the same parameter now agree. (2) STAFF-LOAN REFERENCE RATES REWORDED, NOT RESOLVED. Wage Type Catalogue F359 carried the income-year-2026 reference rates as an unresolved lookup. They are not unresolved: on the pattern of every prior year they are set by a Royal Decree published the following February and cannot exist before February 2027. The cell now says so in those terms and keeps the income-year-2025 rates as the declared provisional basis, together with the standing requirement to recompute the benefit and restate the 281.10 once the definitive rates appear. The disclosure is unchanged in substance; only the wording that made a dated future dependency read as a research gap has gone. (3) DmfA CODE TABLES NOW REACHABLE. Form Reference Guide column D now names the two new BE-DD-001-v1.4 mapper blocks that carry the DmfA remuneration code (zone 00067, Bijlage 7, 44 codes) and the DmfA service code (zone 00062, Bijlage 8, 52 codes). BE_gap_closure_patch_spec.md items 7 and 8 nominated this workbook as the target for those two tables on the belief that a partial table already sat here carrying codes 1-5 and 5/10/12. No such table has ever existed in this workbook, and the WTC structural contract - six sheets, and twenty columns A-T on the catalogue sheet - leaves no place to put one without breaking it. The tables therefore went to the Data Dictionary, which exists to carry code tables, and this workbook points at them. Nothing was duplicated: this sheet's own standing rule is that exactly one copy of a crosswalk is authoritative. NO WAGE TYPE, FORMULA, TAX CODE OR GLOBAL WTC BASELINE ROW WAS TOUCHED IN THIS PASS. | Released | Wallisson Gomes |
|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|  | REMARKS |  |  |  |  |
|  | Topic | Remark |  |  |  |
|  | BUILD ORDER - OUT OF SEQUENCE (deliberate, confirmed) | This catalogue is Stage 5 of the pipeline and WAS built before Stage 3 (Report Specs, BE-compliance/04-reports-spec/) and Stage 4 (Data Dictionary, BE-compliance/05-data-dictionary/), both of which were empty for Belgium at the initial build on 13 August 2026. That was a deliberate, confirmed decision and not an oversight. BOTH STAGES HAVE SINCE BEEN DELIVERED: the nine report specifications were delivered on 14 August 2026 and re-issued on 16 August 2026 - eight at v1.2, BE-NSSO-005-Limosa at v1.3 (DESCOPED); the Data Dictionary was delivered on 14 August 2026 and re-issued as BE-DD-001-v1.3.xlsx on 16 August 2026. Status of the two consequences recorded at the initial build: (1) CLOSED as a false statement, still open as a mapping - the Form Reference Guide sheet no longer asserts that the specifications and the Data Dictionary do not exist; it now records where the crosswalk is maintained, namely column G (Tax Code) of this catalogue for the Belcotax 281.10 box codes and column L (System Mapping) of sheet 4 of each report specification for the report-row mappings, and it is deliberately not duplicated on that sheet. (2) STILL OPEN - NO DATA DICTIONARY ALIGNMENT CROSS-CHECK HAS BEEN PERFORMED FROM THIS CATALOGUE'S SIDE. BE-DD-001-v1.4's own changelog records an alignment patch taken against BE-WTC-001 from the Data Dictionary side on 14 August 2026; that is the other direction and is not a substitute. Related item BE-X10-011, CLOSED 17 August 2026: 78 cells across 4 of the 9 specifications carry a Mercans Code from column A alongside the wage-type name, and the remaining BE-WTC-001 references name the catalogue as a whole or a payroll concept rather than an individual wage type. Column A now holds 359 Mercans Codes. Updated 17 August 2026, Stage 10 cross-artifact fix, finding BE-X10-003. |  |  |  |
|  | LANGUAGE OF THE LOCAL-LANGUAGE COLUMN - DUTCH, WITH FRENCH OUTSTANDING | Belgium has no single national payroll language. Under CCG Module 1.1 the language of every personnel document is imposed by law according to the EMPLOYER'S SEAT OF OPERATION, not the employee's residence: Dutch in the Dutch-language region (Decree of 19 July 1973, sanction NULLITY), French in the French-language region (Decree of 30 June 1982, sanction nullity), the language of the EMPLOYEE in bilingual Brussels-Capital (coordinated laws of 18 July 1966, sanction translation at the employer's expense with retroactive effect), and German in the German-language region. There are NO bilingual personnel documents in Belgium, not even in Brussels: each payslip is unilingual, and an English payslip is never sufficient on its own. DOCUMENTED DEFAULT FOR THIS VERSION: column C is populated in DUTCH as the first-built language. A FRENCH-LANGUAGE VERSION OF COLUMN C IS A MANDATORY SECOND DELIVERABLE AND IS NOT YET BUILT; a German version is required only for employers seated in the German-language region. Do not deploy this catalogue for a Wallonia-seated or Brussels employer until the French column exists. |  |  |  |
|  | SOCIAL SECURITY STRUCTURE - SINGLE FUND, NO PER-FUND COLUMN EXPANSION | Belgian employee social security is ONE global contribution of 13.07% of gross with NO CEILING (globaal beheer), collected by the NSSO and allocated to the branches administratively - pensions 7.50%, medical care 3.55%, sickness and disability 1.15%, unemployment 0.87%. Payroll does NOT configure per-branch subject-to flags. Columns Q (Subject to Social Insurance) and R (Subject to Pension) are therefore used EXACTLY as the CZ template defines them and have NOT been expanded into per-fund columns. Because the first-pillar pension is a branch INSIDE the single global contribution and there is no separate pension deduction in Belgium, column R mirrors column Q wherever the global contribution applies. |  |  |  |
|  | COLUMN SEMANTICS USED IN THIS CATALOGUE | N Subject to Tax: Yes = enters the taxable base for the wage withholding tax; No = statutorily exempt; YES-NO = taxable only in part, where the split depends on a case-by-case legal analysis that payroll cannot pre-determine (used once, on wage type 62182 protection indemnity, where the part repairing NON-PROFESSIONAL damage is not taxable and the part replacing remuneration is); NA = not an income line. \| O Table Tax: Yes = the ordinary Annex III monthly scale (scale I / II / III) applies; No = the ordinary scale does not apply (exceptional payment, or exempt); NA = not an income line. \| P Special Tax: Yes = a separate percentage table or a separate levy applies - the exceptional-remuneration table, the double-holiday-pay table, or separate taxation at the average rate of the last normal year; No = none; NA = not an income line. \| Q Subject to Social Insurance: Yes = within the NSSO wage concept, full employee AND employer contributions; No = outside it; YES-NO = ASYMMETRIC, meaning the employee contribution is due but the employer contribution is not, or ordinary contributions are replaced by a special contribution; NA = not an income line. \| R Subject to Pension: mirrors Q, per the note above. |  |  |  |
|  | WHERE THE ASYMMETRIC "Yes-No" VALUE IS USED | Double holiday pay and the double part of the departure holiday pay (employee 13.07% due, NO employer contributions - the classic Belgian trap); the CBA no. 90 bonus and the purchasing power premium (33% special employer and 13.07% employee solidarity contributions in place of ordinary contributions); the profit premium; the flexi-job wage (28% employer contribution, no employee contribution); student work within the 650-hour quota (2.71% / 5.42% solidarity contributions); the mobility budget pillar-3 cash balance (38.07% special employee contribution); the non-competition indemnity and the SWT/RCC supplement (specific levies). |  |  |  |
|  | SPECIAL CONTRIBUTIONS NOTED IN THE FORMULA COLUMN, NOT IN A NEW COLUMN | The instruction for this build was to note the special social security contribution and the CO2 solidarity contribution against the affected wage types rather than to create new columns. Column D (Wage Type Explanation) is an ORANGE column reserved for the development team and this build populates NO orange column, so those notes have been placed in column F (Formula), which is BLUE and is expressly intended to carry plain text with the 2026 rates. The relevant wage types are BE-001 (special social security contribution), 23672 (employer CO2 solidarity contribution) and 62500 (company car benefit, which is excluded from ordinary contributions precisely because the CO2 contribution replaces them). |  |  |  |
|  | TAX CODE COLUMN - SOURCE AND REVALIDATION REQUIRED | Column G carries the Belcotax-on-web form 281.10 box code (and, where no box code exists, the zone number is cited in the Formula column instead). The codes were taken from the FPS Finance Belcotax-on-web brochure held in BE-compliance/00-official-sources/spec-sources/, which is the INCOME YEAR 2024 edition - the most recent edition in the source set. The 281.10 box codes are stable year on year, but they MUST be revalidated against the income-year-2026 Belcotax brochure before this catalogue is used to drive an actual Belcotax filing. This is a known open item, not an assumption presented as verified. SEPARATOR CONVENTION FOR MULTI-BOX WAGE TYPES: where one wage type reports into more than one box, the box codes are listed in column G separated by a SEMICOLON AND A SPACE, lowest first, and the Formula column states which component goes to which box. Exactly one row uses this today - BE-003 at catalogue row 304, coded '284; 360' - and it is described in the remark 'FISCAL WORK BONUS - DUAL BELCOTAX BOX CODE 284 AND 360' below. The catalogue therefore uses 24 distinct box codes, one more than the 23 recorded at the initial build. |  |  |  |
|  | PARAMETERS THAT MOVE DURING 2026 - CONFIGURE AS DATED VALUES, NOT CONSTANTS | GMMI: EUR 2,154.11 (1 Jan) -> EUR 2,189.81 (1 Apr) -> EUR 2,233.61 (1 Aug, the 2% pivot-index step). Social work bonus - TWO components since 1 April 2024, each with its own limits, maximum and degressive coefficient, added together (P = R_B + R_A); the parameter set steps FIVE times in 2026 (1 Jan, 1 Mar, 1 Apr, 1 Jul, 1 Sep) and two of those steps move a coefficient without moving a maximum. Component B (bediende/white-collar): full amount up to EUR 2,218.73 (1 Jan and 1 Mar) -> EUR 2,255.50 (1 Apr) -> EUR 2,300.62 (1 Jul and 1 Sep); maximum EUR 165.87 -> EUR 168.62 -> EUR 171.99; coefficient 0.2699 throughout; reaches zero at EUR 2,833.36 -> EUR 2,880.32 -> EUR 2,937.93. Component A (bediende/white-collar): full amount up to EUR 2,833.36 (1 Jan and 1 Mar) -> EUR 2,880.32 (1 Apr) -> EUR 2,937.93 (1 Jul and 1 Sep); maximum EUR 123.00 -> EUR 125.04 -> EUR 127.54; coefficient 0.2807 (1 Jan) -> 0.2442 (1 Mar) -> 0.2738 (1 Apr) -> 0.3196 (1 Jul) -> 0.2739 (1 Sep); reaches zero at EUR 3,271.48 (1 Jan) -> EUR 3,336.98 (1 Mar, unchanged 1 Apr and 1 Jul) -> EUR 3,403.62 (1 Sep). Component B's zero point is always component A's full-amount limit, so the two dovetail. The blue-collar (arbeider) maxima AND coefficients are the white-collar figures x 1.08 (e.g. from 1 Jul 2026 EUR 185.75 / 0.2915 and EUR 137.74 / 0.3452) because the contribution runs on the 108% base; the wage limits are identical for both categories, S being the wage at 100%. Annual cap EUR 3,466.44 (1 Jan) -> EUR 3,523.92 (1 Apr) -> EUR 3,594.36 (1 Jul). CLOSED 16 September 2026: the 1 July figure was carried as an open gap while the NSSO instructions covering Q3 2026 were unpublished; they are now live. NSSO administrative instructions edition 2026/3, the werkbonus annex: 'het totaal van de vermindering per werknemer niet meer bedragen dan 3.594,36 EUR per kalenderjaar'. Cross-checked against the component maxima, which reconcile exactly: EUR 171.99 (component B) + EUR 127.54 (component A) = EUR 299.53 per month white-collar, x 12 = EUR 3,594.36. Homeworking allowance: EUR 157.83 -> EUR 160.99 (1 Mar). Voluntary overtime quota: 100/220 hours -> 360 hours (1 Apr), of which the first 240 net. Notice periods: the 65-week table for contracts started to 31 May 2026; capped at 52 weeks from 17 years for contracts started from 1 June 2026; uniform 1-week notice for the first six months of seniority for contracts started from 1 August 2026. Each is applied FROM ITS EFFECTIVE DATE - earlier periods are never recomputed. |  |  |  |
|  | HIGH-WAGE EXEMPTION THRESHOLD - EUR 86,700, NOT EUR 88,434 | The employer basic and wage moderation contributions are not due on the part of the quarterly wage above EUR 86,700 from 1 January 2026, confirmed unchanged at least through mid-2026. The figure EUR 88,434 was investigated during the CCG content review and found UNSUPPORTED; it must not be used. The 1 July 2026 change to this measure excludes paid athletes and professional cyclists from combining it with their sport-specific contribution reduction (Programme Law of 30 May 2026, arts. 109-110) - a population-scope change, not a change in the threshold value. |  |  |  |
|  | ITEMS FLAGGED RATHER THAN GUESSED | (1) SPECIAL SOCIAL SECURITY CONTRIBUTION REFORM - a legislative reform progressively reducing and eventually abolishing the contribution is confirmed to be under way, but the phase-in percentages and the year of conversion to a genuinely individual levy are NOT confirmed against a primary source. BE-001 carries the full 2026 band table and an explicit warning; no reduced percentage has been configured. (2) MOBILITY ALLOWANCE (cash for car, BE-023) - the scheme has a contested legislative history; it is carried with its 281.10 zone but must be verified as still open before use. (3) NON-COMPETITION INDEMNITY LEVY (BE-039) and DECAVA LEVIES (BE-040) - the CCG establishes that a specific levy applies in each case but does not state the rate, so no rate has been invented; both rows carry the mechanism and require the rate to be sourced before configuration. (4) SECTORAL PARAMETERS - end-of-year premium, eco vouchers, sectoral minimum scales, sectoral pension and training funds and index dates are set per JOINT COMMITTEE and cannot be fixed centrally; the relevant rows say so explicitly. |  |  |  |
|  | BLUE / ORANGE CONVENTION APPLIED | POPULATED: A Mercans Code (orange fill retained per the CZ precedent, which populates this same orange column for all 398 of its rows - the fill marks dev-team ownership of final values, not that the column stays empty), B Wage Type Name in English, C Wage Type Name in Local Language, E Category, F Formula, G Tax Code, J Occurrence, N Subject to Tax, O Table Tax, P Special Tax, Q Subject to Social Insurance, R Subject to Pension. LEFT DELIBERATELY EMPTY FOR THE DEVELOPMENT TEAM (ORANGE): D Wage Type Explanation, H Calculation Type, I Prorating Method, K Ignore If 0, L Enforce Setting, M In Period Reconciliation, S YTD Load, T YTD Remarks. Column A carries the Global WTC Pay Element Code for catalogue rows 2 to 301 (the 300 baseline codes, in baseline order, verified against shared/Global_WTC_V1_0.xlsx by diff_wtc_baseline.py) and a Belgium-specific reference (BE-001 to BE-059) for rows 302 to 360. The Global WTC Coverage sheet remains the human-readable cross-reference against the exact English name and catalogue row. |  |  |  |
|  | FISCAL WORK BONUS - DUAL BELCOTAX BOX CODE 284 AND 360 | Wage type BE-003 'Fiscal work bonus - withholding tax reduction' (catalogue row 304) carries Tax Code '284; 360'. PRIMARY SOURCE: the FPS Finance Belcotax-on-web brochure lists the werkbonus as ONE numbered heading with TWO sub-items - 'a) Die in aanmerking komt voor het belastingkrediet van 33,14% ... 284 ... 2.115' and 'b) Die in aanmerking komt voor het belastingkrediet van 52,54% ... 360 ... 2.210' - with XML tags <f10_2115_bonus/> and <f10_2210_workbonus5254/> respectively. Verified in BOTH editions held in BE-compliance/00-official-sources/spec-sources/: the income year 2024 brochure of 06/12/2024 and the income year 2025 brochure of 14/01/2026. WHY ONE WAGE TYPE AND NOT TWO: the fiscal work bonus is a single relief - it is derived entirely from the social work bonus, requires no employee declaration and is capped as ONE annual amount (EUR 765 per calendar year, not indexed, from income year 2025) - so it is configured once and reported into two boxes. The split is by tax-credit rate, not by a second entitlement, and column F of row 304 already stated it. WHAT WAS WRONG: column G carried '284' alone, so the 52.54% portion was never reported on the 281.10. The Belcotax specification named this gap in four places and asked for it to be raised at the next stage gate - BE-FIN-002-Belcotax28110-Spec.xlsx 'Belcotax 281.10 Spec'!L127 and !N127, 'File Structure'!A80 and 'Population Rules'!E14. Raised and closed here; the four Belcotax cells were updated in the same pass. Added 17 August 2026, Stage 10 cross-artifact fix, finding BE-X10-002. |  |  |  |
|  | CO2 SOLIDARITY CONTRIBUTION MULTIPLIER - NOW CITED TO THE LAW, NOT TO PAYROLL COMMENTARY | Wage Type Catalogue F59 previously stated the 2026 multiplier for combustion vehicles ordered from 1 July 2023 as "4 for 2026 (CONFIRMED via" four named payroll and automotive commentators. Four tier-B sources agreeing with each other is not a tier-A source, and the word CONFIRMED asserted that it was. Corrected 2026-08-17 (blocker BE-X10-022): the figure is set by Article 34, 1e of the Law of 25 November 2021 organising the fiscal and social greening of mobility (Belgian Official Gazette 3 December 2021, numac 2021033910), which completes Article 38, par. 3quater, 3e of the Law of 29 June 1981 establishing the general principles of the social security of salaried workers with the full schedule - 2.25 from 1 July 2023, 2.75 from 1 January 2025, 4.00 from 1 January 2026, 5.50 from 1 January 2027 - and separately raises the MINIMUM contribution to a non-indexed EUR 25.99 from 1 January 2026, EUR 28.57 from 2027 and EUR 31.15 from 2028. The same four factors and dates are reproduced in NSSO administrative instructions 2026/2, special contributions, company vehicles. TWO THINGS THE COMMENTARY-SOURCED VERSION DID NOT CARRY, both now in F59: the multiplier applies to the calculated contribution only and NEVER to the minimum, and the earlier 2.75 was not wrong in itself - it is the 2025 value. The value is stated identically in BE-CCG-001-v1.2.docx Module 7.2, which was corrected in the same pass. No other artifact carries the value; the DD, the DmfA spec and the SIR reference the multiplier by its selector, the vehicle order date. |  |  |  |
